- 07.09.26 03:05 [E-1677] [parent: E-1676] [T-1221] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-recover-t1221] RUN: RECOVERED CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION -- T-1221 scheduler already in source, preserved: no semantic SoundManager._pending FIFO for ordinary requests (deque exists but nothing appends to it); STACK_SHORT/COALESCE_HIGH_RATE/EXCLUSIVE_LONG/PREVIEW policies; explicit dedupe_key coalesce gate in _request; immediate short-over-alarm REPLACED_ALARM intent; QSoundEffect fresh-player-per-short with previous short stopped; SND_ASYNC short winsound support (sync flag in _play_winsound/_winsound_play); SND_SYNC long playback worker thread; bounded provenance ring 256; 12-frame cached caller provenance; Ctrl+Q rapid-repeat tests (36x). VERIFIED REMAINING DEFECT: _SerialWavWorker.jobs is an unbounded queue.Queue and EXCLUSIVE_LONG jobs play sync=True, so the worker blocks for the whole long WAV and every later request FIFOs behind it; _stop_exclusive_alarms silences the device but never purges jobs, so hidden transport intent survives a semantic cancel; _pending empty does NOT imply transport empty. Second defect: QSoundEffect EXCLUSIVE_LONG path overwrites _players['__ui__'] without stopping the previous long player (chorus risk). Plan: failing packaged-backend regressions first (2.1-2.5), then latest-wins single-slot worker mailbox + immediate caller-thread SND_ASYNC shorts + one _preempt_current_transport helper + QSoundEffect long REPLACE. - 07.09.26 04:04 [E-1678] [parent: E-1677] [T-1221] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-t1221-done] RUN: T-1221 DONE -- ROOT CAUSE (two, discovered on the real device): (1) winsound's module lock serializes ALL PlaySound calls, so a SND_SYNC long holds the lock for the whole WAV and every later dispatch -- including the interrupt PlaySound(None, 0) -- waits behind it (measured: a stop waited 9010ms behind a 9.5s alarm; that lock, not semantic _pending, is the user's actual FIFO replay report); (2) winsound has NO SND_SYNC constant, so _winsound_play's winsound.SND_SYNC raised AttributeError on real Windows and every long alarm on the packaged path silently FAILED (never played at all). FIX: _SerialWavWorker rebuilt as a latest-wins single-slot mailbox (Condition + one _next_job + epoch; jobs.put FIFO removed; holding-token exposes the taken job); longs dispatch SND_ASYNC and wait out their file duration on the worker WITHOUT holding the device lock (interruptible by submit notify / drop_pending / close); worker pre-start silence + epoch re-check discards superseded intent before it is heard; caller replaces mailbox FIRST then silences, plus a bounded holding-token confirm loop closing the start-vs-stop race; shorts dispatch SND_ASYNC directly on the caller thread (never enter the worker; scaled-cache kept, first-use scale bounded+cached); _preempt_current_transport(discard_pending, silence) is the single preemption owner (shorts also drop parked long intent); QSoundEffect EXCLUSIVE_LONG now REPLACES (stops previous long player, no chorus); stale completion token invariant preserved; _winsound_play fixed to flags=SND_FILENAME|SND_NODEFAULT(+SND_ASYNC when async) since SND_SYNC does not exist. TESTS: uv run pytest tests/test_sound_manager.py -q -> 80 passed (12 new packaged-transport regressions: 2.1 long replacement no-FIFO, 2.2 A/B/C latest-wins, 2.3 short-during-long + parked-intent drop, 2.4 36-press burst 36 dispatches, 2.5 shutdown interrupt+bounded+idempotent, dedupe-never-stops-current, same-file-without-dedupe distinct, stale-completion harmless, transport-FIFO language); 214 passed across 16 sound-caller suites; compileall PASS; ruff clean. WINDOWS (real device, packaged branch forced QSoundEffect=None, dev checkout -- EXE itself not built, no release code touched): A long replacement 0ms; B click-during-long 11.6ms (was 9010ms); C 20 rapid presses 1ms total, no replay; D newest alarm owns device 0-5ms, 3 older cancelled; E exit during long: worker terminated 7ms, bounded join, repeated shutdown safe. Two runs identical. T-1216 supersede note in E-1679. STOP: no release work in this context.
